==============================================================================
CORRESPONDING SOURCE — OPERATING-SYSTEM PACKAGES
==============================================================================

Generated by compliance/tools/collect_os_source.py from the SPDX
documents for the images built for this delivery. Every delivered
machine is built separately; this manifest describes one machine.

Scope     : copyleft+alpine  (copyleft Debian packages, plus every Alpine package because apk ships no licence text)
Files     : 954
Failures  : 0   (10 reported by the run; all 10 resolved by hand — see MANUAL COMPLETION)

MANUAL COMPLETION — 26 Aug 2026
==============================================================================

The collector run reported 10 failures. All 10 have been resolved by
hand and the files are in this tree. Nothing below was accepted on
trust: each archive was checked against the checksum the Alpine
project itself published for that exact version, and the per-file
entries further down carry the result.

Eight upstream archives were re-fetched from Alpine's own distfiles
mirror (distfiles.alpinelinux.org), which keeps the byte-for-byte
archive each aports build actually consumed. The original hosts had
failed for reasons that re-running would not have cleared:

  apk-tools 2.12.9-r3, apk-tools 3.0.6-r0
      gitlab.alpinelinux.org refuses scripted clients, so the run took
      the GitHub mirror instead; GitHub generates its tag archives on
      demand, so those bytes could never match sha512sums=. The
      distfiles copies match it exactly.
  ca-certificates 20230506-r0, ca-certificates 20260611-r0
      same gitlab refusal, and no matching GitHub tag to fall back to.
  busybox 1.37.0-r31
      busybox.net does not complete a TLS handshake from this network.
  libmd 1.2.0-r0
      archive.hadrons.org offers only a TLS version this client rejects.
  zlib 1.2.12-r3
      zlib.net keeps only the current release; 1.2.12 is gone (404).
  libyuv 0.0.1928-r0
      chromium.googlesource.com builds a fresh tarball per request with
      fresh timestamps, so its sha512 differs on every fetch and can
      never match sha512sums=. The APKBUILD's own fetch() skips that
      check for this one file and verifies the UNPACKED TREE instead
      (_libyuvchecksum). That check was run here and matches:
      b92d034854b4b1f5b67ec10aec9ff242c5a46597fc1f02e92feb9defa73fe863
      abd6c45140e54cb4b76a5831830bcaf695eff44c6362faedd47817051683f80d

The remaining two failures were not missing files. busybox's source=
line contains the shell variables $_openrc_files and $_extras_openrc_files,
which the run could not expand. Every file they name was already
collected from the aports directory listing. All 75 files in busybox's
sha512sums= block are present in this tree and verified.

INCOMPLETELY ENUMERATED — READ THIS BEFORE RELYING ON THE ALPINE
ENTRIES BELOW.

The aports directory could not be listed for 17 Alpine package(s), so the files
beside the APKBUILD were taken from its source= line instead.
source= names the patch
series, and those were fetched. It does not name the .initd,
.confd, .pre-install, .post-install or .trigger scripts that sit in
the same directory, and GPL-2 section 3 counts those among "the
scripts used to control compilation and installation".

Most packages have none of those files, so for most of these the
fallback lost nothing. Which ones, however, is precisely what
could not be determined: without the listing there is no way to
tell an empty directory from an unread one. So this is not a claim
that something is missing. It is a statement that for these
packages, completeness was not established.

To close it, re-run compliance/tools/collect_os_source.py with
GITHUB_TOKEN set in the environment. The listing comes from the
GitHub contents API, which allows 60 requests an hour
unauthenticated -- fewer than a delivery has Alpine packages --
and 5000 with a token. The token needs no scopes; it is read only
to raise the rate limit.

    GITHUB_TOKEN was NOT set for this run.

NOT COLLECTED HERE, and why. This tree is complete for what it
claims and no more; read this before citing it as discharging any
obligation.

  206 permissive Debian source packages.
  They owe attribution, not source, and every Debian
  package installs /usr/share/doc/<pkg>/copyright inside
  the delivered image — so their attribution travels with
  the product and their source discharges nothing.

Debian source is the .dsc / .orig.tar / .debian.tar set published for
the exact installed version. Alpine source is the APKBUILD, every file
beside it in the aports directory, and the upstream archive that
APKBUILD patches.

The upstream archive is here for EVERY Alpine package, not only the
copyleft ones. For the copyleft packages it is the corresponding
source and is owed as such. For the permissive ones it is where
their licence text lives: Alpine installs no licence text in the
image for all but 6 of them, so these archives are how the
attribution those packages do owe reaches the recipient. Section
5.9 of THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt states that route; this is it.

The aports ref each APKBUILD was taken from is recorded per package
below. It is the commit apk itself recorded for the installed package
(the c: field of /lib/apk/db/installed), which is the exact tree the
package was built from; where apk recorded no commit, the tag of the
Alpine release the image runs was used, and failing that the stable
branch. Never master. Whichever ref answered, the pkgver in the
APKBUILD was compared against the installed version before the file was
kept, so the recipe corresponds to the binary that shipped.

VERIFICATION. Two different hashes appear per file and they do
different jobs:

  "sha256" is of the file as delivered here. It lets the recipient
  confirm that what is on this machine is what this manifest
  describes. It says nothing about where the bytes came from.

  "verified" names the algorithm used to check the file against a
  checksum published by whoever published the archive -- the hash
  snapshot.debian.org's index returns, the Checksums-Sha256 stanza of
  a .dsc, or the sha512sums= block of the APKBUILD. That check ran
  during collection and a mismatch aborted the package rather than
  being recorded, so no file below carries bytes that contradict its
  publisher. This is what makes the tree corresponding source and not
  merely plausible source: upstream projects do re-roll release
  tarballs under the same filename.

  "verified : no checksum was published for this file" means exactly
  that. The file is delivered and its delivered hash is recorded, but
  no upstream value existed to check it against. It is stated per file
  rather than left as an absence.

  Files taken from the aports tree itself -- the APKBUILD and the
  patches and scripts beside it -- carry no "verified" line, and that
  is not an omission. There is no separate checksum to check them
  against, because the ref they were fetched at IS the guarantee: a
  git commit names its own content, so a file retrieved at the commit
  apk recorded cannot be a different file than the one Alpine built
  from. The ref is on the "ref" line of each Alpine entry.
